## 1. Lifting The Maximally-entangledness Assumption In Robust Self-testing For Synchronous Games

- **Score**: 9.0 / 10  |  **Recommended**: ✅ Yes
- **Source**: Quantum  |  **Feed**: Quantum
- **Direction**: Quantum Information
- **Published**: 2026-10-01
- **Authors**: Matthijs Vernooij And Yuming Zhao
- **Link**: [https://quantum-journal.org/papers/q-2026-10-01-2222/](https://quantum-journal.org/papers/q-2026-10-01-2222/)
- **Tags**: `robust self-testing`  `synchronous games`  `non-local games`  `Quantum Low Degree Test`  `von Neumann algebras`

### TL;DR

The paper lifts the maximally-entangledness assumption in robust self-testing for synchronous games, proving that perfect synchronous games that are robust self-tests under symmetric projective maximally entangled strategies are also robust self-tests for all strategies.

### Motivation

Robust self-testing results in non-local games are often proven under the physically unjustified assumption of symmetric projective maximally entangled (PME) strategies, so these results need to be upgraded to be physically relevant.

### Method

The authors use tracial von Neumann algebras and the framework of synchronous games to prove a general lifting theorem that removes the PME restriction, then apply it to the Quantum Low Degree Test.

### Result

They show that any perfect synchronous game that is a robust self-test when restricted to PME strategies is in fact a robust self-test for all strategies, and obtain an efficient qubit test as an application.

### Conclusion

The work makes previous robust self-testing results for synchronous games physically meaningful and provides a new efficient qubit test via the Quantum Low Degree Test, advancing the theory of device-independent certification.

*Keywords: robust self-testing, synchronous games, non-local games, Quantum Low Degree Test, von Neumann algebras*

## 2. Shifting Erasure Cost Below The Landauer Bound With A Demon-Biased Thermal Bath

- **Score**: 8.7 / 10  |  **Recommended**: ✅ Yes
- **Source**: Phys. Rev. Lett.  |  **Feed**: Physical Review Letters
- **Direction**: Information Thermodynamics
- **Published**: 2026-09-30
- **Authors**: Salambô Dago And Ludovic Bellon
- **Link**: [http://link.aps.org/doi/10.1103/rw6w-bskc](http://link.aps.org/doi/10.1103/rw6w-bskc)
- **Tags**: `Landauer principle`  `Maxwell's demon`  `erasure cost`  `feedback control`  `stochastic thermodynamics`

### TL;DR

This paper experimentally demonstrates that the energetic cost of erasing a one-bit memory can be shifted below the Landauer bound by using a demon-biased thermal bath and feedback-generated virtual potential.

### Motivation

The Landauer principle sets a fundamental lower bound on the energetic cost of erasure for a one-bit memory in thermal equilibrium, but it is important to understand how feedback and non-equilibrium resources can modify this bound.

### Method

The authors implement a micro/nanoscale experiment with a hysteretic bias in a feedback-generated virtual potential, effectively realizing a demon-biased thermal bath, and measure the resulting erasure cost.

### Result

They observe erasure costs below the standard Landauer bound, showing that the bound can be shifted by the biased feedback potential.

### Conclusion

The results extend Landauer's principle to nonequilibrium feedback scenarios and clarify the role of information and Maxwell-demon-like resources in thermodynamic erasure.

*Keywords: Landauer principle, Maxwell's demon, erasure cost, feedback control, stochastic thermodynamics*

### TL;DR

The paper introduces a sufficient condition and testable criterion to certify network nonclassicality that genuinely escapes the shadow of Bell's theorem, with examples in the bilocality scenario.

### Motivation

Network nonlocality often reduces to standard Bell nonlocality, so distinguishing genuinely novel network effects from Bell-type nonlocality is crucial for understanding nonclassical correlations.

### Method

The authors define a sufficient condition for being outside Bell's shadow and a criterion called minimal network nonclassicality, then apply it to the 3-chain/bilocality scenario and construct examples.

### Result

They provide quantum and exotic operational-probabilistic-theory examples of correlations that escape Bell's shadow, including a proof for certain correlations in the bilocality scenario.

### Conclusion

This framework offers a principled way to assess the novelty of network nonclassicality and contrasts with prior approaches, clarifying what is fundamentally new in network nonlocality.

*Keywords: network nonlocality, Bell's theorem, bilocality, quantum nonlocality, minimal network nonclassicality*

### TL;DR

The paper uncovers long-range multiparty entanglement and an infinite hierarchy of entanglement exponents at measurement-induced phase transitions, with exact results via percolation mappings.

### Motivation

Measurement-induced phase transitions exhibit entanglement scaling distinct from equilibrium criticality, but the spatial structure of multiparty entanglement and its universal exponents remain poorly understood.

### Method

The authors introduce entanglement clusters and measure-weighted graphs to characterize average entanglement spread, then use non-unitary conformal field theory and percolation mappings to compute exact exponents in 1D and numerical exponents in 2D.

### Result

They conjecture exponent relations (classical dominance, monotonicity, subadditivity), obtain exact k-party genuine multiparty entanglement exponents for a 1D measurement-only circuit, verify them numerically, and find the first exponents for a 2D MIPT mapping to 3D classical percolation.

### Conclusion

The work provides a firm foundation for understanding multiparty entanglement in MIPTs and more general ensembles of quantum circuits.

*Keywords: measurement-induced phase transitions, multipartite entanglement, genuine multiparty entanglement, percolation, conformal field theory*

Quantum LDPC codes must satisfy orthogonality constraints that typically reduce Tanner graph girth and structurally bound the minimum distance, preventing the classical girth-distance-performance trade-off from directly applying.

### Method

The author uses permutation matrices with controlled commutativity and restricts orthogonality constraints to only the active part of the construction while preserving regular check-matrix structures. This circumvents conventional distance limitations and enables a girth-8, (3,12)-regular quantum LDPC code.

### Result

The constructed code, under BP decoding combined with a low-complexity post-processing algorithm, achieves a frame error rate as low as [MATH] on the depolarizing channel with error probability [MATH].

### Conclusion

The work breaks the orthogonality barrier in quantum LDPC code design, enabling large-girth regular codes that avoid latent low-weight logical operators and exhibit strong decoding performance.

### Result

### Conclusion

### TL;DR

Intense terahertz light drives a flux-Floquet instability in high-temperature superconductors, producing a giant magnetic response that indicates preformed electron pairs persist above Tc.

### Motivation

Understanding whether superconducting pairing survives above the transition temperature is central to cuprate physics, and light-driven (Floquet) states offer a way to probe hidden pairing correlations.

### Method

The authors develop a theoretical treatment of a light-induced flux-Floquet instability in fluctuating superconductors, analyzing the nonlinear magnetic and electromagnetic response to intense terahertz driving.

### Result

The instability yields a giant magnetic response that reveals bound electron pairs surviving well above the superconducting transition temperature.

### Conclusion

Floquet driving provides a route to detect and exploit superconducting fluctuations above Tc, with implications for light-controlled high-temperature superconductivity.

*Keywords: Floquet engineering, high-temperature superconductivity, superconducting fluctuations, terahertz driving, magnetic response*

The paper presents a general construction linking topological loop states (noncontractible loop eigenstates) in exactly flat bands to topological invariants, exemplified by the Lieb and kagome lattice models.

### Motivation

Electronic flat bands host localized Wannier-like zero modes, and understanding how their real-space topological conditions translate into band topological invariants is an open question relevant to the classification of topological flat-band systems.

### Method

The authors analyze localized orbitals in Lieb and kagome models that satisfy a topological condition, deriving noncontractible loop eigenstates in real space and a topological band-touching structure, then generalize this to a construction relating loop states to topological invariants.

### Result

They establish a general mapping from topological loop states to topological invariants in exactly flat bands, showing that the loop eigenstates along a real-space axis encode the nontrivial band topology.

### Conclusion

The work provides a unified real-space framework for characterizing topological flat bands, clarifying the connection between localized loop modes and topological invariants in lattice models.

The authors develop a micromechanical framework for a disordered mean-field model of soft spins to characterize its nonequilibrium replica-symmetry-breaking (RSB) phase, obtaining exact results on its linear response to force monopoles.

### Motivation

Glasses exhibit puzzling low-temperature behavior, and mean-field models with nonequilibrium RSB phases mimic these properties; understanding their local micromechanics could clarify the physics of realistic glasses.

### Method

The authors analyze the linear response of the model to force monopoles, deriving exact analytical results supported by numerical computations to extract collective degrees of freedom and characteristic frequencies.

### Result

They identify collective degrees of freedom in the RSB energy landscape, extract a characteristic frequency, and relate local properties to global susceptibilities, connecting to realistic glass behavior.

### Conclusion

The micromechanical framework reveals key structural and dynamical features of the nonequilibrium RSB phase, offering insights transferable to real glassy systems.

### TL;DR

Fully kinetic simulations reveal a two-stage magnetogenesis mechanism in collisionless ion-electron plasmas, driven by electron and ion Weibel instabilities and a Biermann battery, leading to strong magnetic field amplification.

### Motivation

The origin and amplification of magnetic fields in astrophysical plasmas remain important open problems, especially where collisions are negligible and dynamics span electron-to-ion scales.

### Method

The authors use fully kinetic simulations with continuous large-scale forcing, capturing unprecedentedly large ranges of spatial and temporal scales until nonlinear dynamo saturation.

### Conclusion

This self-consistent kinetic mechanism provides a plausible explanation for robust magnetogenesis in continuously stirred collisionless plasmas and sustains dynamo action until statistical steady state.

- **Tags**: `Marangoni flow`  `laser heating`  `particle-capped droplets`  `interfacial transport`  `thermocapillary flow`

### TL;DR

The paper reports a counterintuitive reversal of Marangoni flow in laser-heated droplets capped with particles, demonstrating that surface-tension-driven flow can be overridden by other transport mechanisms.

### Motivation

Marangoni flow, driven by surface tension gradients, is a ubiquitous interfacial phenomenon whose direction is generally assumed to be from low to high surface tension; the authors investigate conditions under which this rule breaks down.

### Method

Laser irradiation is applied to droplets whose surfaces are capped with particles, and the resulting flow fields are characterized experimentally to identify the mechanism competing with the thermocapillary Marangoni drive.

### Result

Under laser heating, the flow direction is inverted relative to conventional Marangoni expectations, i.e. an 'override' of the surface-tension-gradient-driven transport is observed.

### Conclusion

The findings expand the understanding of interfacial transport in particle-laden droplets and suggest new routes for controlling flow in laser-based microfluidics and printing applications.

*Keywords: Marangoni flow, laser heating, particle-capped droplets, interfacial transport, thermocapillary flow*

- **Tags**: `eco-evolutionary dynamics`  `climate change`  `population persistence`  `quantitative genetics`  `seabird demography`

### TL;DR

An eco-evolutionary forecasting study integrating quantitative genetics, demography, and climate projections shows that climate mitigation prevents extinction of a long-lived seabird far more effectively than evolutionary adaptation.

### Motivation

Species may be unable to evolve rapidly enough to escape extinction under climate change, so it is critical to quantify whether adaptation or mitigation dominates population persistence for long-lived organisms.

### Method

The authors build an eco-evolutionary forecasting framework that couples quantitative genetics and demographic models with climate projections, applying it to a long-lived seabird population.

### Result

Climate mitigation contributes substantially more to population persistence than does evolutionary adaptation, with adaptation alone being insufficient to offset projected climate-driven declines.

### Conclusion

Conservation outcomes for long-lived species depend primarily on reducing greenhouse gas emissions rather than relying on rapid evolutionary rescue.

*Keywords: eco-evolutionary dynamics, climate change, population persistence, quantitative genetics, seabird demography*

- **Tags**: `synaptotagmin 7`  `C2A domain`  `calcium sensing`  `synaptic facilitation`  `memory`

### TL;DR

The C2A domain of synaptotagmin 7 is shown to sense calcium and, via its linker, is required for both synaptic facilitation and memory in neurons.

### Motivation

Synaptic strength is dynamically regulated by patterns of neuronal firing, but the molecular machinery that couples short-term plasticity to longer-term memory processes remains incompletely understood.

### Method

The authors use molecular/physiological perturbation of the synaptotagmin 7 C2A calcium-sensing domain and its linker in neurons, combined with electrophysiological measurements of synaptic facilitation and behavioral memory assays.

### Result

Disrupting calcium sensing by the C2A domain through the linker abolishes synaptic facilitation and impairs memory, indicating that this domain-linker module is essential for both processes.

### Conclusion

Calcium sensing by the synaptotagmin 7 C2A domain, acting via the linker, provides a mechanistic link between short-term synaptic facilitation and memory formation.

*Keywords: synaptotagmin 7, C2A domain, calcium sensing, synaptic facilitation, memory*

Immunotherapy resistance remains a major clinical obstacle, and LRRC15 has been proposed as a marker of a mesenchymal, TGFβ-driven resistance program, but its regulatory basis across cancers is not fully characterized.

The authors combine bioinformatic analysis of cancer genomics/transcriptomics datasets with molecular and pharmacological characterization of TGFβ–LRRC15 regulatory elements across tumor types.

Regulatory components of the TGFβ–LRRC15 axis correlate with disease progression and immunotherapy resistance across diverse cancer cohorts, identifying them as tractable biomarkers and potential therapeutic targets.

- **Tags**: `LHCb`  `B_c+ meson`  `lepton-flavour universality`  `semileptonic decay`  `branching fraction ratio`

### TL;DR

The LHCb Collaboration measures the ratio of semileptonic branching fractions of B_c+ decays into J/ψ τ+ ν_τ versus J/ψ μ+ ν_μ, probing lepton-flavor universality.

### Motivation

Lepton-flavour universality tests in B-meson decays are sensitive probes of physics beyond the Standard Model, and the B_c+ system offers a complementary channel to the well-studied B meson anomalies.

### Method

Using proton-proton collision data recorded by the LHCb detector, the ratio of branching fractions is extracted from a fit to the J/ψ plus lepton kinematic distributions after correcting for detection and selection efficiencies.

### Result

The measured ratio R(J/ψ) is reported with its statistical and systematic uncertainties, providing a test of lepton-flavour universality in B_c+ semileptonic decays.

### Conclusion

The measurement constrains possible new-physics contributions to the b→c τν transition and is consistent, within uncertainties, with the Standard Model expectation.

*Keywords: LHCb, B_c+ meson, lepton-flavour universality, semileptonic decay, branching fraction ratio*

This paper studies the spread of defection in evolutionary Prisoner's Dilemma games on networks using deterministic replicator dynamics with nearest-neighbor coupling.

### Method

They model each network node as a mixed population of cooperators and defectors, with interactions governed by a Prisoner's Dilemma payoff matrix and dynamics given by deterministic replicator equations on graphs.

### Result

The paper likely derives scaling laws and characterizes how network topology affects the invasion and fixation of defection across the population.

### Conclusion

The findings may provide general scaling relationships for the spread of defection in networked evolutionary games, with implications for understanding cooperation in complex systems.

- **Tags**: `inertial confinement fusion`  `laser implosion`  `light-ion spectroscopy`  `OMEGA laser`  `shock-to-compression transition`

### TL;DR

A laser-fusion experiment on OMEGA varies glass-shell thickness to study light-ion species separation as implosions transition from shock-dominated to compression-dominated regimes.

### Motivation

Understanding how nuclear fusion fuel and shell materials mix and separate during implosion is central to improving inertial confinement fusion (ICF) performance.

### Method

Glass-shell gas-filled targets with thicknesses from 4.2 to 9.4 µm were imploded at the OMEGA Laser Facility, and charged-particle spectroscopy of light-ion products was used to track species separation across the shock-to-compression transition.

### Result

The experiments reveal systematic changes in light-ion separation signatures as the implosion drive shifts from shock-dominated to compressive, providing a diagnostic of fuel-shell mixing and stagnation conditions.

### Conclusion

Light-ion spectroscopy offers a sensitive probe of hydrodynamic and mixing physics in ICF implosions, informing target design for future high-gain experiments.

*Keywords: inertial confinement fusion, laser implosion, light-ion spectroscopy, OMEGA laser, shock-to-compression transition*

### TL;DR

The paper identifies a wall-localized instability in pressure-driven viscoelastic channel flows bounded by porous walls, distinct from known elastoinertial and Newtonian porous-wall modes.

### Motivation

Understanding instability mechanisms in viscoelastic flows through porous media is important for applications in polymer processing, oil recovery, and biological flows, yet the coupling between viscoelasticity and porous interfaces remains poorly understood.

### Method

The authors perform a theoretical stability analysis of pressure-driven viscoelastic channel flow with porous walls, likely using linear stability analysis and modal decomposition to isolate the wall-localized mode.

### Result

They uncover a new instability that is localized near the porous wall and arises from the coupling between viscoelastic stresses and the porous interface, differing from the elastoinertial center mode and classical porous-wall mode.

### Conclusion

This work provides a novel route to instability in viscoelastic flows via porous interfaces, expanding the understanding of wall-bounded viscoelastic stability.

*Keywords: viscoelastic flow, instability, porous walls, channel flow, fluid dynamics*

### TL;DR

The paper argues that artificial intelligence cannot substitute for human participants in behavioral research, examining the methodological and validity limits of LLM-based simulated subjects.

### Motivation

As large language models are increasingly proposed as inexpensive, scalable stand-ins for human subjects in behavioral and social science experiments, it is important to assess whether such substitution preserves the validity of research findings.

### Method

The authors present a conceptual and empirical critique comparing AI-simulated responses with human participant data in behavioral research settings, analyzing where the two diverge.

### Result

The analysis shows that AI-generated participants fail to reproduce key features of human behavior, undermining the inferential validity of studies that rely on them as substitutes for human subjects.

### Conclusion

AI cannot serve as a valid replacement for human participants in behavioral research, and researchers should retain human subjects where behavioral validity is at stake.

*Keywords: behavioral research methodology, large language models, synthetic participants, research validity, human subjects*

Independent environmental monitoring has been argued to reduce pollution when governments withhold or obscure domestic air quality data, and the 2020s suspension of the US embassy monitoring program provides a natural experiment to test this claim.

### Method

### TL;DR

A microprotein encoded by the long noncoding RNA NEAT1_1 (PSNEAT1) promotes ribosomal DNA transcription by phase-separating with the WRN helicase.

### Motivation

Long noncoding RNAs have traditionally been assumed to function without encoding proteins, but emerging evidence suggests some harbor small open reading frames producing functional microproteins.

### Method

The authors identify and characterize the NEAT1_1-encoded microprotein PSNEAT1 using molecular and cellular biology techniques, including interaction assays with WRN and analyses of phase-separation behavior and rDNA transcription.

### Result

PSNEAT1 interacts with WRN and undergoes phase separation, driving rDNA transcription, revealing a previously unrecognized protein-coding function for the cancer-associated lncRNA NEAT1_1.

### Conclusion

The findings redefine NEAT1_1 as a coding RNA and uncover a microprotein-mediated, phase-separation-dependent mechanism regulating rDNA transcription in cancer biology.

*Keywords: long noncoding RNA, microprotein, NEAT1, phase separation, rDNA transcription*

### TL;DR

A comprehensive review of theoretical and experimental advances in laser-assisted nuclear decay and nuclear excitation over the past decade.

### Motivation

Laser technology advancements enable new studies in nuclear physics, bridging laser physics, nuclear structure, plasma science, and quantum dynamics.

### Method

Reviews theoretical frameworks (time-dependent Schrödinger equation, Fermi's golden rule) and experimental breakthroughs in laser-induced nuclear excitation of Th, Kr, and Sc.

### Result

Summarizes significant progress in understanding laser-nucleus interactions and manipulating nuclear isomers for optical clocks.

### Conclusion

Provides essential groundwork for future fundamental nuclear science and technological applications.

*Keywords: laser-assisted nuclear decay, nuclear excitation, nuclear isomers, time-dependent Schrödinger equation, Fermi's golden rule*

### Result

LIN-44/Wnt controls neurite pruning via UNC-43/CaMKII and PKC-2/PKC, revealing a specific signaling cascade required for this developmental event.

### TL;DR

This paper describes how inflammatory memory inactivates AMPK-stimulated degradation of DDR1, promoting pancreatic ductal adenocarcinoma growth.

### Motivation

Pancreatic ductal adenocarcinoma has a complex tumor microenvironment, and the mechanisms linking inflammation, metabolism, and stromal remodeling to cancer growth are incompletely understood.

### Method

The authors investigate the molecular interplay between inflammatory signaling, AMPK activity, DDR1 turnover, and pancreatic cancer development, likely using cell and animal models.

### Result

Inflammatory memory prevents AMPK-mediated DDR1 degradation, leading to DDR1 accumulation that supports pancreatic cancer growth.

### Conclusion

Targeting the inflammatory memory–AMPK–DDR1 axis may offer therapeutic opportunities for pancreatic cancer.
